It said: TRUST YOUR WIFE. She thought this an excellent instruction. He thought it suspicious that the handwriting was hers.","Their friends had arranged a treasure hunt across an old spa museum for Selma's birthday. Selma, a cheerful mom-to-be, wanted an evening unrelated to appointments. Her partner Theo wanted to propose, though the ring had vanished into the museum's elaborate preparations. He asked Mara to help track down the box without warning Selma. Cassian, as usual, was informed after agreeing to carry things.","His plan would crystallize into disaster if someone opened the box too soon. Mara offered to focus on the practical problem. Their friends were essentially rehearsing a surprise, not a play, but Theo seemed determined to enact every possible mistake in advance. His self-image as a romantic strategist was at stake, and he found that unbearable.","The length of the map unsettled Theo. He would enquire about it, considering that a surprise should improve the odds of happiness, not the number of doors one had to open.","Oren, the autistic curator, had designed the route. He wanted to allocate clues according to the players' interests, not imitate television's loud escape rooms. Excessive noise could make the evening horrible for him. He had come up with a quiet signal for help. Mara promised to do her share by watching for it. The cultural exchange students helping him were equally determined.","Oren had once used ticket analytics to decide which rooms needed extra seating. Tonight he was de facto master of ceremonies, and he wanted to democratize the evening without surrendering it to noise. His behavioural plan concerned comfort rather than control. The friends agreed to adhere to his signal, a helpful precaution that gave rise to a quieter evening.","The first room examined the industrial revolution. A steam pump stood amidst tools and old bathing equipment. Water once heated to forty degrees centigrade had supplied the spa. A later photograph showed the nearby nuclear plant across the river, its cooling towers pale against the sky. The town's energy history was more complex than its nostalgic postcards suggested.","Contrasting floor tiles marked the route. A restless child from the departing tour wanted to enact a sea battle on them. Oren gently redirected him. Trying to inject enthusiasm into every quiet corner would be futile. The museum had no need to make money from one more attraction at the expense of a visitor's peace.","Theo tried to click on a dark screen. It gave a feeble flash. Oren had warned them that this might not be the case for every terminal: some were deliberately inactive. They must detect the correct one through consecutive dates on the labels. Selma read them calmly while Theo tried to assimilate every instruction at once.","Mara admired the puzzle's precision. The majority of labels carried factual dates, but a baseless rumour about buried gold had been included as a trap. For purposes of the game, a claim needed a named source. Cassian wanted to bury the rumour and move on. His righteous impatience was not helped by Selma's appreciative laughter.","They reached a gallery Oren called anti-happy art. The paintings refused cheerful endings. One contrasted a conventional body ideal with a woman painted heavily and tenderly, her tiredness visible. Another used cosmic scale to make a person seem small without making her worthless. Oren hoped to democratize art discussions by letting visitors describe their reactions before reading his text.","A student tried to intimidate Theo with an abstract interpretation. Mara intervened. The picture should be thought of as an invitation, not an examination. Its meaning could be bound up with someone's experience without requiring everyone to feel the same thing. The student's claim that only sadness showed depth was essentially as unbalanced as compulsory cheerfulness.","Oren would not discriminate between interpretations according to a speaker's confidence. Nor would he accept a racial joke disguised as criticism. He wished the distinction were obvious; in many ways, discussion still required care. Cassian chose not to dwell on the offender once the apology arrived. He offered Selma a positively extravagant bow instead, which threatened to stun Theo into fresh jealousy.","Selma pointed to a key beneath a painted window. The real window's shape matched it. Her longstanding love of small details paid off. She opened a compartment and found another clue beside a letter from the artist's great-grandmother. Since then, the family had preserved the painting's old frame. The paper's texture helped confirm its age, although that alone did not date the words.","“Keep in mind that I am winning,” Selma told Theo.","He smiled awkwardly. “For your sake, I am trying.”","A scientist among their friends, Dr. Joss, joined them from a late train. He had brought a journal with a high impact factor and a terrible mood. Its rejection had hurt his self-image. Oren told him the journal's average citation measure did not decide the merit of his individual work. Tonight he might try to meditate on a cake instead.","Joss initially tried rationally to defend his gloom. Mara asked whether it was not a setback but rather an ending he feared. He had to admit those differed. The humble prospect of revision was the lesser disappointment. His righteous anger could persist all evening or take a chair. He chose the chair and another biscuit.","The next clue would inaugurate a race through the old baths. Theo wanted to innovate by skipping the map. Mara voted against it. “We can vote down your shortcut if necessary.”","He argued to no avail. She owed their progress to Selma's patience and Cassian's compass. A second note fell from the frame: YOUR PRIZE IS IN THE WATER."],"questions":[],"wordCount":964,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0148\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Theo gave a positively desperate smile. The water was shallow, but the duck moved every time he reached for it. Cassian rolled his sleeves. Mara made a private note to thank Oren for this stage of the evening.","The plan had been to allocate points for ingenuity. Amidst the laughter, Theo forgot points and climbed onto the rim. Selma told him to come down. His feeble claim that he was inspecting the tile gave rise to another burst of laughter. Mara steadied the ladder before his haste could add an injury to the birthday.","As usual, Cassian volunteered for the wettest job. The duck's route ran close to the body's ideal balance point, according to Theo, who was inventing science to hide embarrassment. Selma asked whether his body ideal involved a man falling into a fountain. He decided to bury the explanation before it grew worse.","Oren came in, worried by the noise. As an autistic host, he had planned the sensory conditions carefully. The unexpected echoes were tiring. Mara used their quiet signal. Her friends lowered their voices and shifted to the adjoining room. She wanted to do her share without making a ceremony of helping him. He looked appreciative, then explained the bath's pump controls.","The screen's analytics showed water usage, not anyone's intelligence. For purposes of the game, they could click on a diagram to stop the current. Theo had clicked on the decorative duck instead. Selma did not dwell on the distinction. She pressed the right control, and the duck settled.","Two consecutive clicks had moved the duck, and the third stopped it. A contrasting control was labelled RESET. Oren asked them to come up with their own explanation of what each did before trying again. The point was not to intimidate a guest, but to make experimentation safe. Their pleasure should not be thought of as a graded result.","Her result was not a miracle but rather a correct reading of the screen. The puzzle should not discriminate against a player who needed more time to assimilate the instructions. Oren intended to eliminate the distracting image next time. Behavioural observations from these friendly trials would help, though he would not turn five friends into universal evidence.","Oren hoped to inaugurate a regular game night. He wanted to innovate in small, useful ways rather than add excessive gadgets. Some lesser puzzles could be removed. A friend's comfort was at stake; the evening need not make money to justify itself. The cultural exchange students were already helping him choose quieter routes.","His carry-on bag slipped heavily from his shoulder. A ribbon began to sway in the draft. Theo caught it awkwardly, trying to enact the role of untroubled host. Mara thought the attempt futile. His anxious self-image was at odds with Selma's pleasure. She would democratize the hunt herself if he kept deciding which clues she could see.","Theo retrieved the duck. Its lid was empty.","The probability of his proposal going smoothly was shrinking. He tried rationally to inspect the compartment, but his hand shook. Mara could detect that something had changed. His smile did not stun her; his sudden silence did. Selma asked if he was ill.","“I am a horrible liar,” he said.","“You are a versatile one,” Cassian offered. “You have invented four different reasons to climb a bath.”","Oren checked his notes. The box had been moved to the anti-happy art gallery during cleaning. A cultural exchange student might have returned it to storage. The mom-to-be wanted to join the search. Theo said there was no need. She gave him a look that eliminated that suggestion.","The group passed the spa display again. The nuclear plant photograph offered a clue about heat, not hidden radiation. The industrial revolution pump was a different technology. Oren's map asked players to discriminate between sources, and Theo had blended them in his anxiety. His baseless belief that every room required specialist science had made a simple route difficult.","The cosmic mural above the machinery had been painted by a local astronomer. Theo mistook its stars for more clues. Mara thought the hunt had become de facto a test of his anxiety. Its factual labels mattered less to him than an imagined deadline. He should not let that longstanding habit discriminate against his own happiness.","Joss wanted to use the museum's analytics to track down the cleaner. Oren refused to disclose staff movements for a party puzzle. Their safety should be bound up with privacy. Publicity would not be the case here. Joss accepted the boundary. His journal's impact factor was giving him no special authority over an evening.","Selma found a small box behind a cleaning trolley. It contained an old brooch, a gift from her great-aunt, not the ring. The clue had referred to another prize. Theo looked unbalanced enough to sit down. Mara offered him a glass of water at room temperature, twenty degrees centigrade according to the nearby display. She hoped he would not climb into it.","“I owe this confusion to my own ambition,” he admitted.","Mara laughed. His humility was essentially a better beginning than another righteous explanation. “Keep in mind that she already likes you.”","The remaining clue described a place to meditate. Selma knew it at once: the old quiet garden. Since then it had been closed for repairs, but a covered route now led there. The friends set off, strongly in favour of rescuing the evening.","Joss proposed using museum rules to vote down Theo's next shortcut. Oren laughed and asked them to focus on the map instead. Its stringent route avoided loose paving. The needlessness of another vote was apparent to everyone except Theo, who kept trying to find an exception. She stopped beneath a tree whose branches were tied away from a broken wall. The safety notice was stringent. Nobody would cross the barrier merely to create a pretty photograph. Mara found the modesty of that decision unexpectedly comforting after so much grand planning.","Oren had promised to allocate the last clue himself. Amidst the repairs, he had put the ring box on a dry bench behind the door. Its disappearance had been an optical trick: the duck held only a mirror pointing towards the garden symbol. Theo had removed the mirror with the lid and failed to see it. His baseless fear of losing the ring had nearly buried the clue.","The mirror was tilted at an unbalanced angle. He corrected it and invited Joss to click on the garden symbol, making the small arrow light up. Oren's analytics would record that the instruction confused almost everyone. For purposes of improving the puzzle, that mattered more than a single player's feeble excuse. It gave him a concrete change to make.","Mara looked at Cassian. He was trying hard not to laugh. As usual, he was much more convincing before she met his eyes. The two of them withdrew behind a pillar. Their friends did not need an aristocratic audience for a private answer.","Theo began with a rehearsed speech about cosmic coincidence. Selma stopped him. She loved his nervous optimism and his habit of making ordinary days interesting. She did not need him to assimilate every romantic convention at once. The future would be bound up with morning alarms, bills and the child they expected, not only moonlit gardens.","His wish was not a perfect performance but rather a life with her. That thought finally began to crystallize into words. The de facto speechwriter inside his head fell quiet. He would not intimidate himself out of asking. Selma's hand was warm, the air about eighteen degrees centigrade, and the horrible speech no longer seemed necessary.","The mom-to-be took his hands. He asked. She said yes before he reached the final word.","Mara heard the appreciative laugh that followed and pressed a hand over her own smile. Cassian kissed her fingers. The private moment should be thought of as theirs, he murmured. They would not come up with an excuse to interrupt it. They had already done enough to inaugurate the evening's chaos.","Oren stood at the garden door. He had to detect whether the group needed him without intruding. His autistic preference for clear plans had survived a wonderfully unclear night. Mara thanked him without praising his endurance as a spectacle. He said he had enjoyed most of it. In many ways that was a better answer than obligatory delight.","The plan's behavioural assumptions had been modest. They might not be the case for every group, so Oren would ask future visitors what helped. The cultural exchange students suggested clearer language. He agreed to adhere to the simple signals and eliminate one confusing arrow. Nobody needed to enact a little crisis at every door.","Two consecutive crumbs fell from Cassian's biscuit. Mara watched him meditate on their loss with righteous solemnity. His expression could stun a stranger into believing it serious. She knew better and offered him half of hers. He did not discriminate between charity and bribery where her biscuits were concerned.","Joss came to help carry cups. He had put the journal away. He could do his share without needing the evening to reflect well on his career. A journal's impact factor might matter to his work, but it did not measure his usefulness as a friend. He was beginning to look less heavily burdened by the number.","He thought of the evening as a chance to repair his mood. There was plenty of cake, and the surplus would go home with the volunteers. Scarcity of biscuits had been his last remaining anxiety. Mara said that virtue ought to be rewarded, though his talent for eating might not count. He could persist in calling it service if he wished.","Back through the museum, they passed the anti-happy art gallery. Selma stopped at the painted woman. The contrasting images had made her think about the body ideal surrounding pregnancy. Her own changing body was essentially neither an advertisement nor a failure. The picture could give rise to that thought without being a factual guide to her health. Theo listened, holding her hand.","Selma had spent a lifetime resisting racial and bodily categories other people assigned her. Pregnancy did not give the room permission to inject advice into every conversation. Mara supported her. The hope was to democratize the discussion, not to replace one rigid ideal with another. Quite enough was at stake without ranking their bodies beside the pictures.","Oren wanted to democratize the next cultural exchange evening further. Participants would help choose the works. He would not vote down a suggestion merely because it was unusual, though they might collectively vote down excessive noise. To innovate was not to eliminate every longstanding comfort. He wanted visitors to feel free to dwell on one picture and leave another unread.","His versatile helper packed the signs away. Oren hoped the next event would owe its success to everyone feeling able to speak. It was futile to demand universal delight. Some would prefer a lesser role, others a larger one. Their great-grandchildren might find tonight's decorations strange; that would not diminish the pleasure they had made now.","The last light caught the photograph of the nuclear plant. Next door, the industrial revolution machine stood quiet. Joss read the labels properly this time. Since then, the museum had changed how it described the region's energy history; the revision remained visible. A humble correction could be useful. His own paper might yet benefit from one.","Selma rescued a little iced biscuit shaped like her great-grandmother's brooch. She gave half to Theo. He tried to calculate whether the evening had gone positively or catastrophically well. Mara suggested he stop measuring before he ruined his answer. Rationally, the proposal was settled.","Outside, rain began. Cassian opened an umbrella and drew Mara close beneath it. “I owe my excellent judgment to marrying you,” he said.","“Keep in mind that I chose you first.”","His reply did not require a committee. He kissed her while the others hurried past, then let her pull him laughing towards the car."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1069,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0150\" struck Cassian's boot. He looked down, then up at Mara. “I believe this belongs to someone.”","The kidney was foam, painted red-brown, and part of a travelling anatomy show. Its van had lost a rear-door latch on the inland road. Numerous models lay scattered among fallen scenery. A lung hung from a tree limb; a liver balanced on a fence. The show's founder, Dr. Sana Bell, stood beside the van with sweat on her brow and an expression of exhausted disbelief.","Mara ran to help. Cassian caught the lung before it blew into a field. The moment-to-moment attention required by wind and rolling props made conversation brief. A single mother, Sana had built the troupe with two longtime friends. They offered paid work to the jobless and science shows to schools. Today their nucleus of volunteers had become a roadside rescue team.","A cock crowed from the farm beyond the hedge. Its sound made the scene feel deliberately theatrical. Mara carried the kidney back and helped lash the largest crate to a fencepost. The rope would hold it while they repaired the door. An unchecked gust could scatter the organs again. Given all this, Sana wanted to cancel. Her daughter looked ready to cry.","It had come to feed on grain spilled beside the gate. A greedy goose followed it and stole a label. Cassian's back-and-forth pursuit ended in the loss of a shoelace. The sight was stunning enough to distract Nora from tears. She let rip with laughter before noticing her mother. Sana grinned too; nobody would force Nora to help while frightened.","“We can re-make a door fastening,” Cassian said. “The entire programme may be harder.”","The show was called Body vs. Weather. Its supposed futurological framing involved children imagining a future clinic; the anatomy was ordinary present-day science. A postmodern theatrical joke put a talking tongue in charge of the actors. The real tongue's functions were explained too. Mara thought the combination entertaining. Sana feared it would look like a catastrophe instead.","The programme's intricate opening had taken weeks. Sana hated seeing it scattered. Its improvised cast was the lifeblood of a project too small to survive without friendship. She could cultivate that friendship or turn the accident into a humiliating test of loyalty. Her eloquent silence ended in a practical request: help her find the brain.","A painted abdomen lay open on the grass. Mara recovered a bowel segment, then a panel of abdominal muscle. She was careful not to force either into the wrong slot. A model of a neuron had lost its branching extensions. The brain's cortex, packed in a separate box, was intact. A numbered pathway on the board connected the nerve model to the rest of the display.","Cassian returned with a paper tissue and a damp expression. He had walked through a puddle to rescue the lung. His trousers clung to his thigh. Mara's appreciative glance did nothing to improve his composure. He lifted his chin and asked for another task.","Sana sent him after a plastic skull. Mara found a foam bone beneath the van. A pearly strand representing saliva hung from the mouth prop. Beside it were labels for flesh, breast tissue and a model artery. The actual materials mattered because the children would handle them. A loose vein could easily be mistaken for a decorative cord.","The smaller case held a gene display: a DNA strip inside a cell's nucleus. Its lid had remained shut. The hormone cards were dry too, including one on oxytocin and its roles in birth and milk release. No card promised that a hormone explained every emotion. Sana had rightly removed that claim from an earlier draft. The children's questions deserved a thorough answer, not an easy myth.","A smaller transplant display showed why an organ could not simply be exchanged like a machine part. Its missing instruction card was notably important. Sana would adhere to the limits of her training model. She wanted to focus on basic anatomy, not invite children to make medical decisions from a toy. The demonstration should make sense without pretending to make doctors.","Her daughter Nora tried to keep her end up, rescuing labels while the adults lifted. Mara asked whether somebody could meet her need for a dry jacket. Cassian produced his. The sleeves swallowed her hands. She grinned, the catastrophe beginning to fade away.","Mara balanced the crate against her hip. Its painted bone caught on a nail; Cassian freed it before the tear reached the spine board underneath. He suggested they trade the heavy crate for a wheeled case. The exchange would result in fewer strained shoulders, though the goose appeared at odds with every logistical improvement.","Owing to the delay, they would miss the appointed arrival. The mayor, newly in office, had promised to hold the school hall. Sana called him. The children would wait. The news seemed to impart fresh nerve to everyone beside the van.","Incidentally, the mayor had once played the kidney in a school show. Sana thought that explained his sympathy. The inopportune weather would not stop him helping. Hence their next destination remained the hall. Mara admired the vibrant team already rebuilding its plans. A shortage of self-promotion had never been its problem; a shortage of dry rope was more urgent.","A fox slipped from the field and seized a foam toe. Mara saw it first. “Cassian.”","He followed her gaze and sighed. “I shall negotiate.”"],"questions":[],"wordCount":917,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0151\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Mara stopped him before he tried to cultivate a permanent friendship. Wild animals should feed on suitable food, not a duke's travel supplies. The animal vanished into the hedge, leaving the bright toe on the ground. Cassian bowed to its departing tail.","A drizzle began to lash the lane. They needed shelter. The farmer offered a barn after recognizing Sana, who had once treated his sister. His cock announced the arrival of visitors as if he had been expecting them. The wind began to fade away beneath the thick roof. Owing to the damp, they laid the painted lung and liver on dry cloth rather than stack them together.","Nora rested a hand on her belly and said she was hungry. Her mother wanted to meet her need for lunch before another rehearsal. The abdominal panel could wait. A pale model gut lay beneath it, startling the farmer's dog. Mara turned the pearly side down before the animal developed ambitions about supper.","The barn's lifeblood was an elderly tractor that apparently started only when praised. The farmer's wife gave it an eloquent appeal and then kicked the tyre. Cassian tried not to stare. The inopportune machinery delay left more time to repair the crate. Hence he accepted a hammer and got on with it, though the back-and-forth advice was plentiful.","Mara wiped sweat from her brow. Cassian found her a stool and a clean tissue. She had grazed her thigh on a crate and refused to call it serious. He inspected the shallow mark with a concentration that made her forget the cold. Sana reminded him there was a patient older than eight in the room, and his wife laughed.","Her own moment-to-moment attention kept returning to his mouth. That was inconvenient, given the child and the numerous props requiring rescue. She sat on her greedy wish to kiss him again and asked for the tape. He gave it to her, looking pleased. The farmer's wife whispered that young love could radiate heat through a wall.","The farmer's barn held a chest organ, its wooden pipes dusty but intact. Nora asked whether that was the same kind of organ as the kidney. Sana explained the shared word, then asked her to choose the right labels for the anatomy crate. The abdomen and bowel pieces needed cleaning before the children touched them. Nobody would use a visibly dirty prop merely to meet a schedule.","A model limb had lost its joint pin. Cassian made a temporary wooden replacement while Mara repaired the nerve board's loose pathway. The neuron still looked awkwardly lopsided, but its labels were legible. She refused to trade clarity for symmetry. A permanent repair could follow at the workshop. Today they would focus on safe assembly.","Nora worried that her mother's audience would force her to perform while tired. Sana promised they would not force her to do anything. She could keep her end up by helping quietly, or rest. A single mother could need help without making her child the de facto second adult. Mara liked Sana for saying it plainly.","The van's hold contained a stunning tangle of scenery. Its intricate postmodern frame had become a huge obstacle. Sana wanted to phase in lighter panels after this trip. She would adhere to the basic teaching plan and let the more elaborate futurological jokes wait. Body vs. Weather was becoming a documentary. Incidentally, the wind appeared to be winning.","The accident would cast doubt on Sana's transport arrangements, she admitted. It need not cast doubt on every part of the show. She could phase in a safer system rather than struggle against the same broken latch. This distinction helped her make sense of the day. The mayor, still in office after an awkward first month, offered a mechanic's number.","Cassian repaired the door latch. He rightly refused to promise it would survive unchecked use. They could drive slowly to the hall and have a mechanic assess it there. Given all this, Sana accepted that the journey would be late. She could not re-make the last hour by wishing it away.","The farmer helped load the cases. A young worker with a beard admired the gene model. He had left school before learning much biology. Sana showed him the DNA strip in the nucleus, then let him handle the model. The gesture seemed to impart more confidence than a long lecture. The jobless volunteers she trained had often begun with similar questions.","The hormone cards needed drying at the corners. Nora read the oxytocin heading and asked whether the mother in the picture was Sana. It was a stock photograph, Sana explained. Her longtime colleague had chosen it for clarity. She would not use her own patient images for self-promotion. A child could learn about breast tissue without seeing an identifiable patient.","Mara put the tongue and saliva props into separate bags. A bone, an artery and the skull went beside them. Cassian found the final missing vein hanging from his own sleeve. He held it up with imperial dignity. Nora let rip with laughter, and even Sana's tired face began to radiate amusement.","At the door, the farmer handed over a fresh towel. “For the high performer,” he told Cassian.","Mara accepted it for her husband. “He is still in training.”"],"questions":[],"wordCount":896,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0152\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵追赶一颗肾脏</div><h1>A Fox with Excellent Taste</h1><p id=\"chapter-0152-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3412\" data-words=\"3412\">fox</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2591\" data-words=\"2591\">dropped</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8551\" data-words=\"8551\">toe</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9196\" data-words=\"9196\">when</a> Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5733\" data-words=\"5733\">opened</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5916\" data-words=\"5916\">packet</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5667\" data-words=\"5667\">of</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-980\" data-words=\"980\">biscuits</a>.
